Forex market is a distributed system and Trader sometimes engulfs depends on broker's data feed (the question is whether it is intentional or not ) Posts: 143 Thank you received: @By_Tor2014: Yes you are right: extremes of one TF often are midrange of bigger TF. But it is easy to notice, that if you have 4H levels (which are 269 1W/1M midrange) then price will very often reacts on this level. And we can participate in that reaction. Some time ago I noticed than engulfs on which I put my attention in one TF were simply pullback moves into bigger TFs DPs to engulf. So I was getting long where I should go short... :) According to levels above/below levels I know the pain about you are talking. My current view on that topic is: - look for clear and fresh DPs to ENG - FTRs, some are just more clear than others - approach on LTFs - look for big decisions change - I mean if there is a strong pole up, and after that the base of this pole is broken and pole in opposite direction is created then it is a big decision change Look into Michal238 journal, there are mass of information about that topic! The following user(s) said Thank You: Canadough, cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 31 Oct 2014 17:28 #228 antraxia Hey By, Offline I hear your frustration. I am still very frustrated with my progress at times as well. I would say that PA is PA, and is TF independent. You should not be Trader able to tell the difference between a 2 tick chart and a monthly chart. Its all the same reflection of manipulation of emotions. I put more weight on the Measured move trader higher Tfs PA vs the 15 min. I treat the Monthly as a countrie's money and who aspires to simplify the LTFs as pocket change. Who do you think is more comitted to reaching their target? That being said you may be trying to use the engulf as an indicator (I know thats how I used it in the beginning and I was extremely frustrated as well; sometimes it works and sometimes it doesn't arrrrrrgh!) If we simply used the engulf in isolation even the engulf of the previous FTR or pole base we might as well throw the old MACD back on maybe add some moon cycles further. in for good measure :). Posts: 2H1O8 ME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Thank you received: We try to get around this by reading the market. We do this by looking to 253 the engulf of certain important zones or use it as a "heads up" that price may want to relax and test the next fresh level of supply or demand. This does not always result in a useful trade and also doesn't result in a winning trade. Its information that we must weigh to make a decision on whether to be long short or out. So I've attached one of your charts with some mark ups (next post) that may address the questions you will have to answer in order to use the engulf properly. Otherwise we're just dogs running after balls. This is exactly what the ITs want. To keep us jumping at shadows. I'm posting the analysis for those who can't read yet (I'm only moderately good at it but I practice every day) or are just starting. I have left alot out on the chart as this took me a bit of time to write up and double check. Feel free to add to it. If you can do this already I appologise but I gained some good insight to doing this exercise and I encourage everyone else to do the same. Tear the charts apart. Why did this happen? Why did price stop here and not here? We'll suck at it for the first few but eventually light bulbs start to go on. So the lesson to take away from this post for others that dont' have time to read it now, is that an engulf, QM, compression, cap, FTR, PAZ etc by themselves is meaningless without context. You might as well consult an astrologist or have your palm read. You can only draft if->then statments when you have conditions that are met again and again. i.e. If price compresses into HTF demand and there is room down to the next flag and I get an engulf and I get LTF confirmation on the retest then I'll take a trade. However, it is often way more fluid than this. You wil have to study further to understand when you can use discression on your entries if your ideal entry is not present. Hope this helps someone. Cheers. The following user(s) said Thank You: Canadough, cuimingzhao Apprentice Bottle Washer QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Engulf 31 Oct 2014 17:31 #229 antraxia Hindsight glasses fully on (do not discount this analysis as it trains your subconscious to react in realtime). HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Offline Trader Measured move trader I've labeled 4 supplys and 2 demands on the left hand side of the chart as who aspires to simplify we progress down a-f. a:Current top cap further. b:Second FTR (depends on rather it engulfed anything to the left) Posts: 218 c:Third Supply (this is the bottom of stacked supply and serves as the Thank you received: source/origin of the break down d:Fourth FTR/Flaglimit/supply/source of the final leg down (this is a 2TB 253 reaction to a flag on the left) e:Demand source (we don't' know it is until we get and engulf up and start making new highs f:Final demand source 1: Before 1 we can see that price left the supply with good momentum it formed a base rallied up and created a FTB to this supply which may be weak but we've had an engulf so if we get lower TF action and can get good R:R I'll be game for a trade depending on whether that base is in HTF demand or not. Now look at what happens when we touch the base, we get compression and then a burst of comp LQ away from the zone. Ask yourself: does this compression indicate that there are buyers or sellers in this base area? Should we use caution on the retest especially since we have had a FTB already. Now we get the break of the supply (FL/FTR/Origin of the final down move) but we aren't all that surprised when it breaks. Why? Because this area isn't fresh. If this break is true then we're heading up to test the base or sweet FTR of this total leg down. All this has to be in the context of what the higher TF's are doing but lets assume there was HTF demand at the base but the area was wide and we have two distinct demand levels. 2: We flag just below the bottom of my stacked supply origin. We break out test the supply then go back into the flag. We then break out of the bottom of the flag. This is the engulf we make money off of. Look at the room down to the next fresh level. If price bounced before HTF demand we should be drooling and smacking our chops :). So if there was HTF demand before HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT how would we know that it may be safe to go short here besides the engulf. Well the approach. It approached like a mack truck but then it stonewalled then made a weak assed attempt into supply. See those tails. We then reject strongly and then compress can-can like back through the previous bar. So does this compression mean there are buyers or sellers at this level. When we revisit will this still be the case? 3: Ok we've had our engulf so we're all ready for a long. I've got my lot size figured out, I'm planning my trip to OZ and polishing my pimp ring when I say wait a minute. Wasn't there untested compression away from this demand. What does that mean? Maybe I'll monitor PA on approach (yes if I set a TT here due to the engulf overnight I got burned). Well we see that price is coming on like a banshee with big bear bars just like the approach above but the difference is that this zone has been tested once and we have compression that hasn't vacuumed price back down yet. Our expectation is for price to possibly test the next fresh level of demand. So we wait for LTF PA on this one. The smart version of me (the one with out the pimp ring who drives a volvo) smiles in delight as price smashes through this demand engulfing it. 4: Ok so where is the next opportunity for a trade. Well that FL @ 4 looks nice and we sort of have a flaggy approach feel but without LTF signal I'm going to pass especially if we haven't engulfed HTF demand. I'm not good enough at these sorts of trades yet but thats ok. More around the corner. 5: Lets say this hits the secondary HTF demand. It is rejected and there is a small FL that led to the final push down. It gets eaten on its way to 4 again but I'm not sold on a trade here unless we get really close to the base low as the R:R is bad for the first target. 6: With this engulf 5 becomes the new base. 4 isn't a fresh zone and the DP to break the zone was only OK. It also creates a stacked flag so we could look for longs here on return depending on approach and PA. The last Flag before the engulf of the flag limit gets tested twice before the trip to the sweet FTR at 7. So we make a note of this. 7: We're expecting the move off this FTR as its fresh and its Les Paul's sweet one. i.e. The first one generated after an engulf of the last leg up. We're ping ponging (happens when we're trapped in a PAZ until one of the limits gets broken) so I'm only looking for nice PA confirmation trades as zones are getting used up fast. 8: We get a vicious bar down from 7 and even an engulf of a low but we fail to engulf that rotten flag that seems to be stubbornly holding price up. There is no room down as flags are stacked until 11. So I'm waiting. 9: Price wanders on up with some zeal to 9. This is 2TB to the original supply zone but FTB to 2 after an engulf which makes this the base of the last leg down and probably an origin on a HTF. Hey its 5:00 somewhere HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT right so lets trade. Well what have we got. We've had an engulf down into HTF (assumption) so supply may have to be removed here before price can continue up. The approach is good (running out of steam slightly compressed). We have a QM from 2. My desired setup isn't really here on this TF. We have some of the pieces and after the spike down it gives us an opportunity to enter at the cap but it has met its target FL @10 and it didn't really engulf it so price may want higher (I'm not a senior yet so I can only read at my level). So I pass on this one. 10: I inform George Bush I was left behind. Price now eats this FL and compresses down to 8 where it is held up again. We are suspicious of longs as the next fresh level is still 11 below. 11: It spikes down to 11 and jonts up to the tip of our compression to test it. You could argue that 11 is the sweet FTR for this leg up and if PA is evident you could take a risk for a trade up to the top of compression. This is made trickier on further observation as it appears that we are compressed right down to the base if you include the stacked flags on the initial move up. We know that compression vacuums price so we haven't reached the target yet. 12: We race up to 12, flag just before it (which becomes the DP to break 12), break 12 and flag again (which becomes the DP to continue up to 13) Why did this move happen? Well it appears compressed down from 9 with only one real supply pocket which is acknowledged briefly on the way up. 13: We are coming off great strength but are showing weakness to the fresh supply @13. I can see my implied engulf cap retest trade here. We have a compressed move up, the big red doji eats the compression and misses our first target. Go short at the retest of the cap supply. Now we're aware that price may only want the next fresh (demand/flag/FTR/flag limit) but we have an ace up our sleeve. This move that confused us (well at least it confused me on my first attempt to explain it) may be trying to trick us as our compression target was not met. So that means this welfare 1:1 possibly 2:1 trade has the potential to be a spanking test of the origin of compression. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 27 Nov 2013 08:44 #52 Andyfx The Engulf - FTR combo is one of my favorite trade setup for a quick scalp. Offline If we are moving away from an HTF SD zone, I wait for an Engulf of previous high/low, then I take the trade if the FTR touch the SD pocket at Trader the origin of the engulf. In fact it is trading SD pockets of a CP zone , subsequents Engulf and FTR to SD pocket at the origin of the Engulf. Like it is the FTB to previous FTR again and again after an engulf. Posts: 72 Just make sure of not buying near supply and selling near demand, and Thank you received: that the SD pocket is tight and clean with a strong move away making the engulf. Look to the left and take the trade only if there is free space or even 107 better an engulf of previous opposite FTR SD pocket into a new PAZ, or wait for an Engulf of a FL and enter on the second FTR wich is FTBing the FTR SD pocket. The QM is also a powerfull Engulf-FTR setup. Edit: an other option to trade ENG+ FTR combo: in strong momentum trends, price will often bounce at SR flip instead of origin-of-the-engulf-SD- pocket, if we are not sure we can split the position in 2: open one at SR flip and one if the price touch SD pocket, same stop loss above SD pocket for HOME both. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Engulf 06 Dec 2013 15:06 #144 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Aaron Pucci, Offline You have raised some really good points regarding the engulf, I think the post you're talking about was written by Pedini, I have saved some of his Associate Trader charts and look at them daily. Posts: 668 I will have a crack at showing my way of thinking with regards to the engulf. Thank you received: When I began looking for engulfs at certain areas, I initially was looking for FL to be engulfed, then when I read a post, I think again from Pedini's 1707 thread, where he took a trade as a low created the HH was engulfed, so he shorted the RT to the cap. So things began to click in my head, and I started seeing important areas that needed to be engulfed to consider a trade, and when you look back at some of my charts and some seniors, I think most of the lows or highs are actually FLs or FTRs that have been engulfed. If you look on the hourly chart of my 1st trade you may see that the H that caused the LL was in fact an FTR also, if this makes sense. I am not 100 percent sure I am correct, but this mind set I have acquired over the few hundred charts I have created seems to make sense to me, wait for a senior to comment would be the safest bet. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 11 Dec 2013 06:15 #161 HotRod HOME EDDafUt tCraAdTeIrO1Nwrote: HFi OPuRcUcMi, MORE LOGOUT Offline For me, Hotrod has done a fantastic job in taking the words right out of my mouth, cheers buddy. I have attached the charts to try and Trader show my thinking. Posts: 108 On the first chart I have circled where I believe price did engulf Thank you received: lows to qualify this as an FTR, IMO it engulfed two lows, this is a good thing. 189 DT I didn't want the above in bold to confuse anyone. I have sat here and wrote a few different explanations, but then erased them because they just don't reconcile what I have in my brain. Does a base have to engulf lows to be an FTR? An FTR is created when a H/L or S/D zone gets engulfed and then price retraces. IF says the FTR is the deepest part of the retrace. So how do you have a deepest part of the retrace if a base never engulfs that last low (in this case) or zone? If it doesnt engulf the last low, then the only other alternative is it keeps retracing. Which means you still don't have an FTR. So, yes I guess a base does have to engulf L/Hs or zones to be an FTR. Otherwise you don't have a "deepest part" yet. And if price never stops retracing, then you didn't have an engulf, you had a FO. I need to clarify a few things on the following chart, and stop confusing everyone. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT You still have to look at what an FTR engulfed, and what it didn't, to see how strong it is. But it does at least have to engulf that last L/H in the retrace to qualify as an FTR. If that is all that it engulfs then any trade taken should not be expected to go further than the retrace itself, and best planned on a lower TF. But look at that bottom yellow line, if FTR3 had engulfed that low, then the potential RR of any trade taken at FTR3 is much better. Of course then you need to look and see where any HTF demand might be. Its relatively easy to see where price will react. Its the strength of the reaction that is so hard to judge. What an FTR engulfs, and the way price behaves on its way back to that FTR is all we have to judge that reaction. Look at the reaction after hitting demand, the break of that high, and the pockets of demand left behind on the way back to FTR3 is a lot to overcome for any short. Sorry for any confusion I have created, and hopefully I cleared it up. Any seniors feel free to correct me. Posts: 554 Dear Druwainwright, Thank you received: 1510 price ALWAYS make new highs when approaching SUPPLY, doesn't it? Good telltale sign that a level will hold is COMPRESSION, I strongly recommend you to do your homework on it . If the price does not approach SUPPY / DEMAND in a compressed way, then you should wait for confirmation in the form of the engulf of last demand on the way up or last supply on the way down. This will give you a hint that price really wanna turn. And for me, personally, this is not enough. I like to wait for the first FTR, then it is usually a good trade upon return. For more info u can look at my journal p. 13. Trader There are few things that we must take into account when looking for a Posts: 143 trade opportunity: Thank you received: 1) HTF: where we are going to? In my opinion price wanted FTR above your trades (I have the zones drawn 269 earlier, before answering your post, so I don't cheat :P). Personally I will wait for price reaction on this level, not earlier. So according to charts price is going from one FTR to the one above, so at the time you take your trades it was in "midrange". Which is not good because price is very vulnerable for fakeouts. Additionally you taken the trades "counter trend" because price was going from demand to supply. 2) Some time ago I realize one very simple stuff: when price is at supply and I would like to take a short trade, then probably there are many other traders which want to do the same. So when there are so many traders shorting, then they are at big boys aim, because if they (big boys) also want to short at current supply level, then they must fill their big orders. So it is almost sure that the FO will happen near levels like that. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Generally we have two scenarios: If there is a CP to a given level we can place pending order. But if there is no CP at the approach, we must wait for flag engulf. I'm not good for the second one yet, so personally I often wait for engulf confirmation - I wait for FTB to FTR which was just created. 3) Sometimes diamonds happens :P And sometimes engulf happened before price reaches proper supply/demand, so you must be aware of levels above your supply/demand levels So you see, there are many, many variables we must be aware of. In my opinion it is not only waiting for a break of the last low/high. We must learn to read the market: where the price is (HTF!!! LTF), where the price is probably going to, what is below/above a given level, is the level strong or it is tested? Maybe price just CP from your level not shot down with big momentum (very often hidden in the LTFs), we must take into account approach (demands/supplies tested on price's way? are there still fresh DPs?). Hopefully we can all learn something from me getting it wrong a little more Hey By, Make a note of why certain FTRs are marked and others are not in KKB's chart. Then ask yourself why is that? I did a study regarding the engulf of previous H and L to the left to get the new more difficult to fake edges of the new PAZ zones. This is not a magic bullet but it gives you a heads up to which FTRS are better as not all are created the same. For instance if we have engulfed a previous PAZ edge as in the chart above, made a new FTR and are heading up we should expect most of the FTRs to break on the way up until we get to our target origin i.e. the next PAZ edge not the next 4H FTR. It has to be made after engulfing another zone to the left. Thats why IF speaks of engulfing the bottom of poles. finding compression etc on a chart. It makes us aware of the zones that price is coming from and going to. There are only extremes i.e. the edges of our PAZ all the rest HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT is chop and prone to FOs. I would stick with HTF zones as if you try this on the Hourly or lower its a bit tricker to sort out and the big moves are coming off the HTFs anyways. Now you bring up a good point that what is midrange on the weekly can provide whopper scalps or daily swing trades. We just have to be aware that if price is bouncing from 1H demand to 1H supply while its on its way to 4H demand we can only expect definite pips to the next flag in the 1H TF. it could fake us and then go the other way or continue on past the 1H target but we have to keep an eye on PA: compression into demand, where could comp LQ go to, is this demand truly fresh, is there an untested flag directly below our entry, are there any patterns etc. There is a quote that I remember and recite daily from IF: Something to the effect that we need PA to prove that there is still supply or demand at any given level. Now we see wild 100:1 R:R trades taken from perfect 5 pip 1M levels but either PA was given on a larger TF or we are watching the approach like a Hawk and manage the trade as it progresses. The seniors I'm sure have a better grasp of PAZs than we do so they can pinpoint untested S/D at the edge of zones with great precision. We will be able to do this one day too. Sorry for my rambling hopefully this is helping. If you want to TT levels you have to weave all the TFs together to see if they are in sync. Then I have to have proof in the form of an engulf of a pole base with room to the next flag before I'll take a trade in a HTF zone. Yes I don't get 50 trades a week but I'm finding the ones I do get are much better quality. Cheers. The best ones we'll cover involve an Engulf Offline Simply put, an engulf involves price making a lower low on reaction to Supply, or higher high from Demand Chief Cook and Bottle Washer This is a very powerful sign that price is on the turn. They say that a trend is a succession of LL's and HH's, or vice versa, so if you get a Lower Low from Supply, it's a great first sign that the uptrend may be over, and of course flip the scenario for an HH from Demand. If you see a piece of truth glistening in the dirt, get down on your knees and gently dig it out, for it's the greatest treasure you will ever find Posts: 3576 Thank you received: 14517 If you didn't have the patience to wait for that engulf, see the Lower timeframe for another lovely engulf showing the way much earlier! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Can you trade an engulf as a breakout trade? Well, yes, but price very often retraces after the engulf, to confuse the breakout traders and hit their stop losses, creating more orders to take the other side of the big money's new direction trades. This means that the stop loss would, in most cases, still need to be beyond the original zone. Here's a video about the engulf https://mega.nz/#!xE4y1RDZ!7nKqcpGga4DyrfoqtiR70IFBuRYean8bS2fPCTeaTEA (passwo rd: RTM2018) So, in essence, it's as simple as this Two of our favourite Price Action structures based on the engulf are the Quasimodo and the Diamond The following user(s) said Thank You: aus1way, valtrg, pedrosa, gjv, bulbuli, Experiorz, fourtiwinks, khanahesan1985, Satan, bestgmx, Jurij, Enlightenme, Al3cs, salivan, pmbGUNNER, alirezat, Shantala, Canadough, Nytewyre, amateur.trader With time, patience and experience, what once seemed very complex becomes very simple indeed Please join www.theblue.community THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 26 Apr 2013 01:49 #2 HotochHiOnME In tEhDe UupCpAeTr lIeOvNel, how wFoOuRldUyMou knowMthOatRpErice wasLgOoGinOg UtoTretrace to a QM level? Forex market is a distributed system and Trader sometimes engulfs depends on broker's data feed (the question is whether it is intentional or not ) Posts: 143 Thank you received: @By_Tor2014: Yes you are right: extremes of one TF often are midrange of bigger TF. But it is easy to notice, that if you have 4H levels (which are 269 1W/1M midrange) then price will very often reacts on this level. And we can participate in that reaction. Some time ago I noticed than engulfs on which I put my attention in one TF were simply pullback moves into bigger TFs DPs to engulf. So I was getting long where I should go short... :) According to levels above/below levels I know the pain about you are talking. My current view on that topic is: - look for clear and fresh DPs to ENG - FTRs, some are just more clear than others - approach on LTFs - look for big decisions change - I mean if there is a strong pole up, and after that the base of this pole is broken and pole in opposite direction is created then it is a big decision change Look into Michal238 journal, there are mass of information about that topic! Posts: 2H1O8 ME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Thank you received: We try to get around this by reading the market. We do this by looking to 253 the engulf of certain important zones or use it as a "heads up" that price may want to relax and test the next fresh level of supply or demand. This does not always result in a useful trade and also doesn't result in a winning trade. Its information that we must weigh to make a decision on whether to be long short or out. So I've attached one of your charts with some mark ups (next post) that may address the questions you will have to answer in order to use the engulf properly. Otherwise we're just dogs running after balls. This is exactly what the ITs want. To keep us jumping at shadows. I'm posting the analysis for those who can't read yet (I'm only moderately good at it but I practice every day) or are just starting. I have left alot out on the chart as this took me a bit of time to write up and double check. Feel free to add to it. If you can do this already I appologise but I gained some good insight to doing this exercise and I encourage everyone else to do the same. Tear the charts apart. Why did this happen? Why did price stop here and not here? We'll suck at it for the first few but eventually light bulbs start to go on. So the lesson to take away from this post for others that dont' have time to read it now, is that an engulf, QM, compression, cap, FTR, PAZ etc by themselves is meaningless without context. You might as well consult an astrologist or have your palm read. You can only draft if->then statments when you have conditions that are met again and again. i.e. If price compresses into HTF demand and there is room down to the next flag and I get an engulf and I get LTF confirmation on the retest then I'll take a trade. However, it is often way more fluid than this. You wil have to study further to understand when you can use discression on your entries if your ideal entry is not present. Hope this helps someone. Cheers. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Offline Trader Measured move trader I've labeled 4 supplys and 2 demands on the left hand side of the chart as who aspires to simplify we progress down a-f. a:Current top cap further. b:Second FTR (depends on rather it engulfed anything to the left) Posts: 218 c:Third Supply (this is the bottom of stacked supply and serves as the Thank you received: source/origin of the break down d:Fourth FTR/Flaglimit/supply/source of the final leg down (this is a 2TB 253 reaction to a flag on the left) e:Demand source (we don't' know it is until we get and engulf up and start making new highs f:Final demand source 1: Before 1 we can see that price left the supply with good momentum it formed a base rallied up and created a FTB to this supply which may be weak but we've had an engulf so if we get lower TF action and can get good R:R I'll be game for a trade depending on whether that base is in HTF demand or not. Now look at what happens when we touch the base, we get compression and then a burst of comp LQ away from the zone. Ask yourself: does this compression indicate that there are buyers or sellers in this base area? Should we use caution on the retest especially since we have had a FTB already. Now we get the break of the supply (FL/FTR/Origin of the final down move) but we aren't all that surprised when it breaks. Why? Because this area isn't fresh. If this break is true then we're heading up to test the base or sweet FTR of this total leg down. All this has to be in the context of what the higher TF's are doing but lets assume there was HTF demand at the base but the area was wide and we have two distinct demand levels. 2: We flag just below the bottom of my stacked supply origin. We break out test the supply then go back into the flag. We then break out of the bottom of the flag. This is the engulf we make money off of. Look at the room down to the next fresh level. If price bounced before HTF demand we should be drooling and smacking our chops :). So if there was HTF demand before HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT how would we know that it may be safe to go short here besides the engulf. Well the approach. It approached like a mack truck but then it stonewalled then made a weak assed attempt into supply. See those tails. We then reject strongly and then compress can-can like back through the previous bar. So does this compression mean there are buyers or sellers at this level. When we revisit will this still be the case? 3: Ok we've had our engulf so we're all ready for a long. I've got my lot size figured out, I'm planning my trip to OZ and polishing my pimp ring when I say wait a minute. Wasn't there untested compression away from this demand. What does that mean? Maybe I'll monitor PA on approach (yes if I set a TT here due to the engulf overnight I got burned). Well we see that price is coming on like a banshee with big bear bars just like the approach above but the difference is that this zone has been tested once and we have compression that hasn't vacuumed price back down yet. Our expectation is for price to possibly test the next fresh level of demand. So we wait for LTF PA on this one. The smart version of me (the one with out the pimp ring who drives a volvo) smiles in delight as price smashes through this demand engulfing it. 4: Ok so where is the next opportunity for a trade. Well that FL @ 4 looks nice and we sort of have a flaggy approach feel but without LTF signal I'm going to pass especially if we haven't engulfed HTF demand. I'm not good enough at these sorts of trades yet but thats ok. More around the corner. 5: Lets say this hits the secondary HTF demand. It is rejected and there is a small FL that led to the final push down. It gets eaten on its way to 4 again but I'm not sold on a trade here unless we get really close to the base low as the R:R is bad for the first target. 6: With this engulf 5 becomes the new base. 4 isn't a fresh zone and the DP to break the zone was only OK. It also creates a stacked flag so we could look for longs here on return depending on approach and PA. The last Flag before the engulf of the flag limit gets tested twice before the trip to the sweet FTR at 7. So we make a note of this. 7: We're expecting the move off this FTR as its fresh and its Les Paul's sweet one. i.e. The first one generated after an engulf of the last leg up. We're ping ponging (happens when we're trapped in a PAZ until one of the limits gets broken) so I'm only looking for nice PA confirmation trades as zones are getting used up fast. 8: We get a vicious bar down from 7 and even an engulf of a low but we fail to engulf that rotten flag that seems to be stubbornly holding price up. There is no room down as flags are stacked until 11. So I'm waiting. 9: Price wanders on up with some zeal to 9. This is 2TB to the original supply zone but FTB to 2 after an engulf which makes this the base of the last leg down and probably an origin on a HTF. Hey its 5:00 somewhere HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT right so lets trade. Well what have we got. We've had an engulf down into HTF (assumption) so supply may have to be removed here before price can continue up. The approach is good (running out of steam slightly compressed). We have a QM from 2. My desired setup isn't really here on this TF. We have some of the pieces and after the spike down it gives us an opportunity to enter at the cap but it has met its target FL @10 and it didn't really engulf it so price may want higher (I'm not a senior yet so I can only read at my level). So I pass on this one. 10: I inform George Bush I was left behind. Price now eats this FL and compresses down to 8 where it is held up again. We are suspicious of longs as the next fresh level is still 11 below. 11: It spikes down to 11 and jonts up to the tip of our compression to test it. You could argue that 11 is the sweet FTR for this leg up and if PA is evident you could take a risk for a trade up to the top of compression. This is made trickier on further observation as it appears that we are compressed right down to the base if you include the stacked flags on the initial move up. We know that compression vacuums price so we haven't reached the target yet. 12: We race up to 12, flag just before it (which becomes the DP to break 12), break 12 and flag again (which becomes the DP to continue up to 13) Why did this move happen? Well it appears compressed down from 9 with only one real supply pocket which is acknowledged briefly on the way up. 13: We are coming off great strength but are showing weakness to the fresh supply @13. I can see my implied engulf cap retest trade here. We have a compressed move up, the big red doji eats the compression and misses our first target. Go short at the retest of the cap supply. Now we're aware that price may only want the next fresh (demand/flag/FTR/flag limit) but we have an ace up our sleeve. This move that confused us (well at least it confused me on my first attempt to explain it) may be trying to trick us as our compression target was not met. So that means this welfare 1:1 possibly 2:1 trade has the potential to be a spanking test of the origin of compression. If that doesn't make us sit up and take interest we are in the wrong gig :). 14: We hit our measly first flag and then compress up away from it (we suspect what this means given the current context weak buyers) and it then blows downward forming a flag @ 11 compressing nicely into a FTB to demand. We could look for longs here up to the next flag if we have PA confirmation but it is the 2TB to what I've assumed was HTF demand. However, the approach is good, our FL has already been tested once so it may be worth the risk to hold longer. 15:We've finished off our chart. We are traveling towards our HTF target and have eaten all the supplies in our way. If 15 were our HTF S then I may be looking for shorts due to the compressed look on the way up. However HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT we've engulfed it and price has just bounced off the origin of the DP to break it. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 27 Nov 2013 08:44 #52 Andyfx The Engulf - FTR combo is one of my favorite trade setup for a quick scalp. Offline If we are moving away from an HTF SD zone, I wait for an Engulf of previous high/low, then I take the trade if the FTR touch the SD pocket at Trader the origin of the engulf. In fact it is trading SD pockets of a CP zone , subsequents Engulf and FTR to SD pocket at the origin of the Engulf. Like it is the FTB to previous FTR again and again after an engulf. Posts: 72 Just make sure of not buying near supply and selling near demand, and Thank you received: that the SD pocket is tight and clean with a strong move away making the engulf. Look to the left and take the trade only if there is free space or even 107 better an engulf of previous opposite FTR SD pocket into a new PAZ, or wait for an Engulf of a FL and enter on the second FTR wich is FTBing the FTR SD pocket. The QM is also a powerfull Engulf-FTR setup. Edit: an other option to trade ENG+ FTR combo: in strong momentum trends, price will often bounce at SR flip instead of origin-of-the-engulf-SD- pocket, if we are not sure we can split the position in 2: open one at SR flip and one if the price touch SD pocket, same stop loss above SD pocket for HOME both. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 06 Dec 2013 03:13 #134 dcabfx It's interesting how many people find S/D through Sam Seiden. Definitely a good start but unfortunately if we took every fresh S/D setup, you would pound your head asking why many of the trades failed. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 11 Dec 2013 06:15 #161 HotRod HOME EDDafUt tCraAdTeIrO1Nwrote: HFi OPuRcUcMi, MORE LOGOUT Offline For me, Hotrod has done a fantastic job in taking the words right out of my mouth, cheers buddy. I have attached the charts to try and Trader show my thinking. Posts: 108 On the first chart I have circled where I believe price did engulf Thank you received: lows to qualify this as an FTR, IMO it engulfed two lows, this is a good thing. 189 DT I didn't want the above in bold to confuse anyone. I have sat here and wrote a few different explanations, but then erased them because they just don't reconcile what I have in my brain. Does a base have to engulf lows to be an FTR? An FTR is created when a H/L or S/D zone gets engulfed and then price retraces. IF says the FTR is the deepest part of the retrace. So how do you have a deepest part of the retrace if a base never engulfs that last low (in this case) or zone? If it doesnt engulf the last low, then the only other alternative is it keeps retracing. Which means you still don't have an FTR. So, yes I guess a base does have to engulf L/Hs or zones to be an FTR. Otherwise you don't have a "deepest part" yet. And if price never stops retracing, then you didn't have an engulf, you had a FO. I need to clarify a few things on the following chart, and stop confusing everyone. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT You still have to look at what an FTR engulfed, and what it didn't, to see how strong it is. But it does at least have to engulf that last L/H in the retrace to qualify as an FTR. If that is all that it engulfs then any trade taken should not be expected to go further than the retrace itself, and best planned on a lower TF. But look at that bottom yellow line, if FTR3 had engulfed that low, then the potential RR of any trade taken at FTR3 is much better. Of course then you need to look and see where any HTF demand might be. Its relatively easy to see where price will react. Its the strength of the reaction that is so hard to judge. What an FTR engulfs, and the way price behaves on its way back to that FTR is all we have to judge that reaction. Look at the reaction after hitting demand, the break of that high, and the pockets of demand left behind on the way back to FTR3 is a lot to overcome for any short. Sorry for any confusion I have created, and hopefully I cleared it up. Any seniors feel free to correct me. Posts: 554 Dear Druwainwright, Thank you received: 1510 price ALWAYS make new highs when approaching SUPPLY, doesn't it? Good telltale sign that a level will hold is COMPRESSION, I strongly recommend you to do your homework on it . If the price does not approach SUPPY / DEMAND in a compressed way, then you should wait for confirmation in the form of the engulf of last demand on the way up or last supply on the way down. This will give you a hint that price really wanna turn. And for me, personally, this is not enough. I like to wait for the first FTR, then it is usually a good trade upon return. At the start I would like to emphasis, that my answer is only my point of view and please consider it rather as a seed for discussion than a correct answer. Offline It would be great if someone from senior members there could comment this post :) Especially my zones. Trader There are few things that we must take into account when looking for a Posts: 143 trade opportunity: Thank you received: 1) HTF: where we are going to? In my opinion price wanted FTR above your trades (I have the zones drawn 269 earlier, before answering your post, so I don't cheat :P). Personally I will wait for price reaction on this level, not earlier. So according to charts price is going from one FTR to the one above, so at the time you take your trades it was in "midrange". Which is not good because price is very vulnerable for fakeouts. Additionally you taken the trades "counter trend" because price was going from demand to supply. 2) Some time ago I realize one very simple stuff: when price is at supply and I would like to take a short trade, then probably there are many other traders which want to do the same. So when there are so many traders shorting, then they are at big boys aim, because if they (big boys) also want to short at current supply level, then they must fill their big orders. So it is almost sure that the FO will happen near levels like that. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Generally we have two scenarios: If there is a CP to a given level we can place pending order. But if there is no CP at the approach, we must wait for flag engulf. I'm not good for the second one yet, so personally I often wait for engulf confirmation - I wait for FTB to FTR which was just created. 3) Sometimes diamonds happens :P And sometimes engulf happened before price reaches proper supply/demand, so you must be aware of levels above your supply/demand levels So you see, there are many, many variables we must be aware of. In my opinion it is not only waiting for a break of the last low/high. We must learn to read the market: where the price is (HTF!!! LTF), where the price is probably going to, what is below/above a given level, is the level strong or it is tested? Maybe price just CP from your level not shot down with big momentum (very often hidden in the LTFs), we must take into account approach (demands/supplies tested on price's way? are there still fresh DPs?). Hope this helps :) Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: aus1way, muzani, antraxia, charlespips, Canadough, cuimingzhao Please read my posts with caution :) Learner trader here :) THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 30 Oct 2014 00:51 #218 muzani theres no engulf.... check out 1 minute FL... the low of the fl was 96.895 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT correct me if Im wrong please... Offline So that being said here's my interpretation of the chart. My data does not match yours at all so I'll just work from your chart. It may not totally answer Trader your question but most times we have to read the PA around the engulf and preface it with the HTF to surmise if its significant or not. Measured move trader who aspires to simplify further. Posts: 218 Thank you received: 253 1: We start a leg down that manages to break your white line this will be our base. Its also a smaller ftr as it engulfed a local low but I can't see if it engulfed anything to the left. At any rate its our base. 2: We break the last flag and the one below it to form a small flag of our HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT own, technically this is a ftr also. 3:There are two parts to this PA, the actual decision to break the low and then it retests back up making the FL=FTR and then engulfs the previous low confirming it is now supply. 4: Price tests up FTB and removes some supply 5: Price slices back up eating the FL and testing the first FTR before the origin. Les Pauls sweet FTR. Price has engulfed the FL for sure but it bounced off the sweet FTR and did not engulf it. I'm looking for a trade that has room to move and it has also not engulfed the origin (remember we like to see bases of poles engulfed for a heads up on a possible reversal). So as it stands now we have the FL engulfed but no room up to the next PAZ. It has instead bounced and is heading back down. We need more PA to figure out what nonsense the big guys are up to. 6: Buggers! They engulf what we thought should be demand. So now 5 is our FL right. But supply is cleared above it so the next fresh supply will be the origin. 7: Price flys up to the origin after acknowledging 5 with a slight kink. We then travel back down. Since I don't know if the bottom of the chart is HTF demand H4 or higher I can't decide if I would take a shot at that nice small doji long after the recent engulf higher on your chart. Price looks like it still aggressively wants to go down but there may be room for a scalp up and the R:R may work. It really depends on whether we engulfed anything to the left with the move down and what zone we are in. I missed some of the PA here but hopefully you get the gist. In short I would wait until 1 and maybe the stacked demand above was just taken out before I would consider a long in this case. I will not take any level without a confirmation engulf and retest of the cap, or sweet FTR, or QM, compression after engulf etc. This engulf has to be the base of a pole with room up to the next flag in HTF S/D otherwise I'm just not interested. I'm not 100% on weaving the proper engulfs together as we go up in TF. I know that HTF engulfs will trump the lower action so I try to be aware of these as well. Does this help you any? Hopefully we can all learn something from me getting it wrong a little more Hey By, Make a note of why certain FTRs are marked and others are not in KKB's chart. Then ask yourself why is that? I did a study regarding the engulf of previous H and L to the left to get the new more difficult to fake edges of the new PAZ zones. This is not a magic bullet but it gives you a heads up to which FTRS are better as not all are created the same. For instance if we have engulfed a previous PAZ edge as in the chart above, made a new FTR and are heading up we should expect most of the FTRs to break on the way up until we get to our target origin i.e. the next PAZ edge not the next 4H FTR. It has to be made after engulfing another zone to the left. Thats why IF speaks of engulfing the bottom of poles. finding compression etc on a chart. It makes us aware of the zones that price is coming from and going to. There are only extremes i.e. the edges of our PAZ all the rest HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT is chop and prone to FOs. I would stick with HTF zones as if you try this on the Hourly or lower its a bit tricker to sort out and the big moves are coming off the HTFs anyways. Now you bring up a good point that what is midrange on the weekly can provide whopper scalps or daily swing trades. We just have to be aware that if price is bouncing from 1H demand to 1H supply while its on its way to 4H demand we can only expect definite pips to the next flag in the 1H TF. it could fake us and then go the other way or continue on past the 1H target but we have to keep an eye on PA: compression into demand, where could comp LQ go to, is this demand truly fresh, is there an untested flag directly below our entry, are there any patterns etc. There is a quote that I remember and recite daily from IF: Something to the effect that we need PA to prove that there is still supply or demand at any given level. Now we see wild 100:1 R:R trades taken from perfect 5 pip 1M levels but either PA was given on a larger TF or we are watching the approach like a Hawk and manage the trade as it progresses. The seniors I'm sure have a better grasp of PAZs than we do so they can pinpoint untested S/D at the edge of zones with great precision. We will be able to do this one day too. Sorry for my rambling hopefully this is helping. If you want to TT levels you have to weave all the TFs together to see if they are in sync. Then I have to have proof in the form of an engulf of a pole base with room to the next flag before I'll take a trade in a HTF zone. Yes I don't get 50 trades a week but I'm finding the ones I do get are much better quality. Cheers. Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Canadough, cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 31 Oct 2014 15:30 #225 bulbuli HOME Hey Tor, FORUM MORE LOGOUT EDUCATION I really understand all the confusion you might be having without clearly understanding the basics or purpose behind the 'Engulf' price action. I was trying to figure out the same initially and had written a small post of my understandings here (might help you a little ) www.readthemarket.com/index.php/forum/jo...ve-bulbuli-s-journal (Post # 13) Offline To put it simply 'Engulf' signifies consumption of orders and thus to have important/significant engulf, we must have a zone (significant) marked Trader where lot of pending orders are expected. But when this level is engulfed it shows that the orders have been consumed and thus price is free to If Life got a little bit go in the direction of engulf (After Retrace Of course !) Milkeyway had a harder, it probably line diagram drawn here : means you just leveled www.readthemarket.com/index.php/forum/tr...708-engulf?start=192 UP !! The best ones we'll cover involve an Engulf Offline Simply put, an engulf involves price making a lower low on reaction to Supply, or higher high from Demand Chief Cook and Bottle Washer This is a very powerful sign that price is on the turn. They say that a trend is a succession of LL's and HH's, or vice versa, so if you get a Lower Low from Supply, it's a great first sign that the uptrend may be over, and of course flip the scenario for an HH from Demand. If you see a piece of truth glistening in the dirt, get down on your knees and gently dig it out, for it's the greatest treasure you will ever find Posts: 3576 Thank you received: 14517 If you didn't have the patience to wait for that engulf, see the Lower timeframe for another lovely engulf showing the way much earlier! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Can you trade an engulf as a breakout trade? Well, yes, but price very often retraces after the engulf, to confuse the breakout traders and hit their stop losses, creating more orders to take the other side of the big money's new direction trades. This means that the stop loss would, in most cases, still need to be beyond the original zone. Here's a video about the engulf https://mega.nz/#!xE4y1RDZ!7nKqcpGga4DyrfoqtiR70IFBuRYean8bS2fPCTeaTEA (passwo rd: RTM2018) So, in essence, it's as simple as this Two of our favourite Price Action structures based on the engulf are the Quasimodo and the Diamond The following user(s) said Thank You: aus1way, valtrg, pedrosa, gjv, bulbuli, Experiorz, fourtiwinks, khanahesan1985, Satan, bestgmx, Jurij, Enlightenme, Al3cs, salivan, pmbGUNNER, alirezat, Shantala, Canadough, Nytewyre, amateur.trader With time, patience and experience, what once seemed very complex becomes very simple indeed Please join www.theblue.community THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 26 Apr 2013 01:49 #2 HotochHiOnME In tEhDe UupCpAeTr lIeOvNel, how wFoOuRldUyMou knowMthOatRpErice wasLgOoGinOg UtoTretrace to a QM level? But it is easy to notice, that if you have 4H levels (which are 269 1W/1M midrange) then price will very often reacts on this level. And we can participate in that reaction. Some time ago I noticed than engulfs on which I put my attention in one TF were simply pullback moves into bigger TFs DPs to engulf. So I was getting long where I should go short... :) According to levels above/below levels I know the pain about you are talking. My current view on that topic is: - look for clear and fresh DPs to ENG - FTRs, some are just more clear than others - approach on LTFs - look for big decisions change - I mean if there is a strong pole up, and after that the base of this pole is broken and pole in opposite direction is created then it is a big decision change Look into Michal238 journal, there are mass of information about that topic! I put more weight on the Measured move trader higher Tfs PA vs the 15 min. I treat the Monthly as a countrie's money and who aspires to simplify the LTFs as pocket change. Who do you think is more comitted to reaching their target? That being said you may be trying to use the engulf as an indicator (I know thats how I used it in the beginning and I was extremely frustrated as well; sometimes it works and sometimes it doesn't arrrrrrgh!) Posts: 2H1O8 ME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Thank you received: We try to get around this by reading the market. We do this by looking to 253 the engulf of certain important zones or use it as a "heads up" that price may want to relax and test the next fresh level of supply or demand. This does not always result in a useful trade and also doesn't result in a winning trade. Its information that we must weigh to make a decision on whether to be long short or out. So I've attached one of your charts with some mark ups (next post) that may address the questions you will have to answer in order to use the engulf properly. Otherwise we're just dogs running after balls. This is exactly what the ITs want. To keep us jumping at shadows. I'm posting the analysis for those who can't read yet (I'm only moderately good at it but I practice every day) or are just starting. I have left alot out on the chart as this took me a bit of time to write up and double check. Feel free to add to it. If you can do this already I appologise but I gained some good insight to doing this exercise and I encourage everyone else to do the same. Tear the charts apart. Why did this happen? Why did price stop here and not here? We'll suck at it for the first few but eventually light bulbs start to go on. So the lesson to take away from this post for others that dont' have time to read it now, is that an engulf, QM, compression, cap, FTR, PAZ etc by themselves is meaningless without context. You might as well consult an astrologist or have your palm read. You can only draft if->then statments when you have conditions that are met again and again. i.e. If price compresses into HTF demand and there is room down to the next flag and I get an engulf and I get LTF confirmation on the retest then I'll take a trade. However, it is often way more fluid than this. You wil have to study further to understand when you can use discression on your entries if your ideal entry is not present. Hope this helps someone. Cheers. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Offline Trader Measured move trader I've labeled 4 supplys and 2 demands on the left hand side of the chart as who aspires to simplify we progress down a-f. a:Current top cap further. b:Second FTR (depends on rather it engulfed anything to the left) Posts: 218 c:Third Supply (this is the bottom of stacked supply and serves as the Thank you received: source/origin of the break down d:Fourth FTR/Flaglimit/supply/source of the final leg down (this is a 2TB 253 reaction to a flag on the left) e:Demand source (we don't' know it is until we get and engulf up and start making new highs f:Final demand source 1: Before 1 we can see that price left the supply with good momentum it formed a base rallied up and created a FTB to this supply which may be weak but we've had an engulf so if we get lower TF action and can get good R:R I'll be game for a trade depending on whether that base is in HTF demand or not. Now look at what happens when we touch the base, we get compression and then a burst of comp LQ away from the zone. Ask yourself: does this compression indicate that there are buyers or sellers in this base area? Should we use caution on the retest especially since we have had a FTB already. Now we get the break of the supply (FL/FTR/Origin of the final down move) but we aren't all that surprised when it breaks. Why? Because this area isn't fresh. If this break is true then we're heading up to test the base or sweet FTR of this total leg down. All this has to be in the context of what the higher TF's are doing but lets assume there was HTF demand at the base but the area was wide and we have two distinct demand levels. 2: We flag just below the bottom of my stacked supply origin. We break out test the supply then go back into the flag. We then break out of the bottom of the flag. This is the engulf we make money off of. Look at the room down to the next fresh level. If price bounced before HTF demand we should be drooling and smacking our chops :). So if there was HTF demand before HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT how would we know that it may be safe to go short here besides the engulf. Well the approach. It approached like a mack truck but then it stonewalled then made a weak assed attempt into supply. See those tails. We then reject strongly and then compress can-can like back through the previous bar. So does this compression mean there are buyers or sellers at this level. When we revisit will this still be the case? 3: Ok we've had our engulf so we're all ready for a long. I've got my lot size figured out, I'm planning my trip to OZ and polishing my pimp ring when I say wait a minute. Wasn't there untested compression away from this demand. What does that mean? Maybe I'll monitor PA on approach (yes if I set a TT here due to the engulf overnight I got burned). Well we see that price is coming on like a banshee with big bear bars just like the approach above but the difference is that this zone has been tested once and we have compression that hasn't vacuumed price back down yet. Our expectation is for price to possibly test the next fresh level of demand. So we wait for LTF PA on this one. The smart version of me (the one with out the pimp ring who drives a volvo) smiles in delight as price smashes through this demand engulfing it. 4: Ok so where is the next opportunity for a trade. Well that FL @ 4 looks nice and we sort of have a flaggy approach feel but without LTF signal I'm going to pass especially if we haven't engulfed HTF demand. I'm not good enough at these sorts of trades yet but thats ok. More around the corner. 5: Lets say this hits the secondary HTF demand. It is rejected and there is a small FL that led to the final push down. It gets eaten on its way to 4 again but I'm not sold on a trade here unless we get really close to the base low as the R:R is bad for the first target. 6: With this engulf 5 becomes the new base. 4 isn't a fresh zone and the DP to break the zone was only OK. It also creates a stacked flag so we could look for longs here on return depending on approach and PA. The last Flag before the engulf of the flag limit gets tested twice before the trip to the sweet FTR at 7. So we make a note of this. 7: We're expecting the move off this FTR as its fresh and its Les Paul's sweet one. i.e. The first one generated after an engulf of the last leg up. We're ping ponging (happens when we're trapped in a PAZ until one of the limits gets broken) so I'm only looking for nice PA confirmation trades as zones are getting used up fast. 8: We get a vicious bar down from 7 and even an engulf of a low but we fail to engulf that rotten flag that seems to be stubbornly holding price up. There is no room down as flags are stacked until 11. So I'm waiting. 9: Price wanders on up with some zeal to 9. This is 2TB to the original supply zone but FTB to 2 after an engulf which makes this the base of the last leg down and probably an origin on a HTF. Hey its 5:00 somewhere HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT right so lets trade. Well what have we got. We've had an engulf down into HTF (assumption) so supply may have to be removed here before price can continue up. The approach is good (running out of steam slightly compressed). We have a QM from 2. My desired setup isn't really here on this TF. We have some of the pieces and after the spike down it gives us an opportunity to enter at the cap but it has met its target FL @10 and it didn't really engulf it so price may want higher (I'm not a senior yet so I can only read at my level). So I pass on this one. 10: I inform George Bush I was left behind. Price now eats this FL and compresses down to 8 where it is held up again. We are suspicious of longs as the next fresh level is still 11 below. 11: It spikes down to 11 and jonts up to the tip of our compression to test it. You could argue that 11 is the sweet FTR for this leg up and if PA is evident you could take a risk for a trade up to the top of compression. This is made trickier on further observation as it appears that we are compressed right down to the base if you include the stacked flags on the initial move up. We know that compression vacuums price so we haven't reached the target yet. 12: We race up to 12, flag just before it (which becomes the DP to break 12), break 12 and flag again (which becomes the DP to continue up to 13) Why did this move happen? Well it appears compressed down from 9 with only one real supply pocket which is acknowledged briefly on the way up. 13: We are coming off great strength but are showing weakness to the fresh supply @13. I can see my implied engulf cap retest trade here. We have a compressed move up, the big red doji eats the compression and misses our first target. Go short at the retest of the cap supply. Now we're aware that price may only want the next fresh (demand/flag/FTR/flag limit) but we have an ace up our sleeve. This move that confused us (well at least it confused me on my first attempt to explain it) may be trying to trick us as our compression target was not met. So that means this welfare 1:1 possibly 2:1 trade has the potential to be a spanking test of the origin of compression. If that doesn't make us sit up and take interest we are in the wrong gig :). 14: We hit our measly first flag and then compress up away from it (we suspect what this means given the current context weak buyers) and it then blows downward forming a flag @ 11 compressing nicely into a FTB to demand. We could look for longs here up to the next flag if we have PA confirmation but it is the 2TB to what I've assumed was HTF demand. However, the approach is good, our FL has already been tested once so it may be worth the risk to hold longer. 15:We've finished off our chart. We are traveling towards our HTF target and have eaten all the supplies in our way. If 15 were our HTF S then I may be looking for shorts due to the compressed look on the way up. However HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT we've engulfed it and price has just bounced off the origin of the DP to break it. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 27 Nov 2013 08:44 #52 Andyfx The Engulf - FTR combo is one of my favorite trade setup for a quick scalp. Offline If we are moving away from an HTF SD zone, I wait for an Engulf of previous high/low, then I take the trade if the FTR touch the SD pocket at Trader the origin of the engulf. In fact it is trading SD pockets of a CP zone , subsequents Engulf and FTR to SD pocket at the origin of the Engulf. Like it is the FTB to previous FTR again and again after an engulf. Posts: 72 Just make sure of not buying near supply and selling near demand, and Thank you received: that the SD pocket is tight and clean with a strong move away making the engulf. Look to the left and take the trade only if there is free space or even 107 better an engulf of previous opposite FTR SD pocket into a new PAZ, or wait for an Engulf of a FL and enter on the second FTR wich is FTBing the FTR SD pocket. The QM is also a powerfull Engulf-FTR setup. Edit: an other option to trade ENG+ FTR combo: in strong momentum trends, price will often bounce at SR flip instead of origin-of-the-engulf-SD- pocket, if we are not sure we can split the position in 2: open one at SR flip and one if the price touch SD pocket, same stop loss above SD pocket for HOME both. FORUM MORE LOGOUT EDUCATION Something like this FTR to FTR SD pocket FTR to SRFlip Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Aaron, bulbuli, Canadough, cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 27 Nov 2013 08:51 #53 Aaron I think this post could be very beneficial for members who have found themselves confused with the whole 'FTR = FL' thing.... QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Engulf 06 Dec 2013 15:06 #144 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Aaron Pucci, Offline You have raised some really good points regarding the engulf, I think the post you're talking about was written by Pedini, I have saved some of his Associate Trader charts and look at them daily. Posts: 668 I will have a crack at showing my way of thinking with regards to the engulf. Thank you received: When I began looking for engulfs at certain areas, I initially was looking for FL to be engulfed, then when I read a post, I think again from Pedini's 1707 thread, where he took a trade as a low created the HH was engulfed, so he shorted the RT to the cap. So things began to click in my head, and I started seeing important areas that needed to be engulfed to consider a trade, and when you look back at some of my charts and some seniors, I think most of the lows or highs are actually FLs or FTRs that have been engulfed. If you look on the hourly chart of my 1st trade you may see that the H that caused the LL was in fact an FTR also, if this makes sense. I am not 100 percent sure I am correct, but this mind set I have acquired over the few hundred charts I have created seems to make sense to me, wait for a senior to comment would be the safest bet. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Larry Levin THANK YOU Engulf 11 Dec 2013 06:15 #161 HotRod HOME EDDafUt tCraAdTeIrO1Nwrote: HFi OPuRcUcMi, MORE LOGOUT Offline For me, Hotrod has done a fantastic job in taking the words right out of my mouth, cheers buddy. I have attached the charts to try and Trader show my thinking. Posts: 108 On the first chart I have circled where I believe price did engulf Thank you received: lows to qualify this as an FTR, IMO it engulfed two lows, this is a good thing. 189 DT I didn't want the above in bold to confuse anyone. I have sat here and wrote a few different explanations, but then erased them because they just don't reconcile what I have in my brain. Does a base have to engulf lows to be an FTR? An FTR is created when a H/L or S/D zone gets engulfed and then price retraces. IF says the FTR is the deepest part of the retrace. So how do you have a deepest part of the retrace if a base never engulfs that last low (in this case) or zone? If it doesnt engulf the last low, then the only other alternative is it keeps retracing. Which means you still don't have an FTR. So, yes I guess a base does have to engulf L/Hs or zones to be an FTR. Otherwise you don't have a "deepest part" yet. And if price never stops retracing, then you didn't have an engulf, you had a FO. I need to clarify a few things on the following chart, and stop confusing everyone. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT You still have to look at what an FTR engulfed, and what it didn't, to see how strong it is. But it does at least have to engulf that last L/H in the retrace to qualify as an FTR. If that is all that it engulfs then any trade taken should not be expected to go further than the retrace itself, and best planned on a lower TF. But look at that bottom yellow line, if FTR3 had engulfed that low, then the potential RR of any trade taken at FTR3 is much better. Of course then you need to look and see where any HTF demand might be. Its relatively easy to see where price will react. Its the strength of the reaction that is so hard to judge. What an FTR engulfs, and the way price behaves on its way back to that FTR is all we have to judge that reaction. Look at the reaction after hitting demand, the break of that high, and the pockets of demand left behind on the way back to FTR3 is a lot to overcome for any short. Sorry for any confusion I have created, and hopefully I cleared it up. Any seniors feel free to correct me. Posts: 554 Dear Druwainwright, Thank you received: 1510 price ALWAYS make new highs when approaching SUPPLY, doesn't it? Good telltale sign that a level will hold is COMPRESSION, I strongly recommend you to do your homework on it . If the price does not approach SUPPY / DEMAND in a compressed way, then you should wait for confirmation in the form of the engulf of last demand on the way up or last supply on the way down. This will give you a hint that price really wanna turn. And for me, personally, this is not enough. I like to wait for the first FTR, then it is usually a good trade upon return. Posts: 217 Thank you received: 213 Attachments: HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: Canadough, cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 30 Oct 2014 00:47 #217 kkb Hi! At the start I would like to emphasis, that my answer is only my point of view and please consider it rather as a seed for discussion than a correct answer. Offline It would be great if someone from senior members there could comment this post :) Especially my zones. Trader There are few things that we must take into account when looking for a Posts: 143 trade opportunity: Thank you received: 1) HTF: where we are going to? In my opinion price wanted FTR above your trades (I have the zones drawn 269 earlier, before answering your post, so I don't cheat :P). Personally I will wait for price reaction on this level, not earlier. So according to charts price is going from one FTR to the one above, so at the time you take your trades it was in "midrange". Which is not good because price is very vulnerable for fakeouts. Additionally you taken the trades "counter trend" because price was going from demand to supply. 2) Some time ago I realize one very simple stuff: when price is at supply and I would like to take a short trade, then probably there are many other traders which want to do the same. So when there are so many traders shorting, then they are at big boys aim, because if they (big boys) also want to short at current supply level, then they must fill their big orders. So it is almost sure that the FO will happen near levels like that. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Generally we have two scenarios: If there is a CP to a given level we can place pending order. But if there is no CP at the approach, we must wait for flag engulf. I'm not good for the second one yet, so personally I often wait for engulf confirmation - I wait for FTB to FTR which was just created. 3) Sometimes diamonds happens :P And sometimes engulf happened before price reaches proper supply/demand, so you must be aware of levels above your supply/demand levels So you see, there are many, many variables we must be aware of. In my opinion it is not only waiting for a break of the last low/high. We must learn to read the market: where the price is (HTF!!! LTF), where the price is probably going to, what is below/above a given level, is the level strong or it is tested? Maybe price just CP from your level not shot down with big momentum (very often hidden in the LTFs), we must take into account approach (demands/supplies tested on price's way? are there still fresh DPs?). Hope this helps :) Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: aus1way, muzani, antraxia, charlespips, Canadough, cuimingzhao Please read my posts with caution :) Learner trader here :) THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 30 Oct 2014 00:51 #218 muzani theres no engulf.... check out 1 minute FL... the low of the fl was 96.895 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT correct me if Im wrong please... Measured move trader who aspires to simplify further. Posts: 218 Thank you received: 253 1: We start a leg down that manages to break your white line this will be our base. Its also a smaller ftr as it engulfed a local low but I can't see if it engulfed anything to the left. At any rate its our base. 2: We break the last flag and the one below it to form a small flag of our HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT own, technically this is a ftr also. 3:There are two parts to this PA, the actual decision to break the low and then it retests back up making the FL=FTR and then engulfs the previous low confirming it is now supply. 4: Price tests up FTB and removes some supply 5: Price slices back up eating the FL and testing the first FTR before the origin. Les Pauls sweet FTR. Price has engulfed the FL for sure but it bounced off the sweet FTR and did not engulf it. I'm looking for a trade that has room to move and it has also not engulfed the origin (remember we like to see bases of poles engulfed for a heads up on a possible reversal). So as it stands now we have the FL engulfed but no room up to the next PAZ. It has instead bounced and is heading back down. We need more PA to figure out what nonsense the big guys are up to. 6: Buggers! They engulf what we thought should be demand. So now 5 is our FL right. But supply is cleared above it so the next fresh supply will be the origin. 7: Price flys up to the origin after acknowledging 5 with a slight kink. We then travel back down. Since I don't know if the bottom of the chart is HTF demand H4 or higher I can't decide if I would take a shot at that nice small doji long after the recent engulf higher on your chart. Price looks like it still aggressively wants to go down but there may be room for a scalp up and the R:R may work. It really depends on whether we engulfed anything to the left with the move down and what zone we are in. I missed some of the PA here but hopefully you get the gist. In short I would wait until 1 and maybe the stacked demand above was just taken out before I would consider a long in this case. I will not take any level without a confirmation engulf and retest of the cap, or sweet FTR, or QM, compression after engulf etc. This engulf has to be the base of a pole with room up to the next flag in HTF S/D otherwise I'm just not interested. I'm not 100% on weaving the proper engulfs together as we go up in TF. I know that HTF engulfs will trump the lower action so I try to be aware of these as well. Does this help you any? But if there is no CP at the approach, we must wait for ag engulf. I'm not good for the second one yet, so personally I often wait for engulf con rmation - I wait for FTB to FTR which was just created. 3) Sometimes diamonds happens :P And sometimes engulf happened before price reaches proper supply/demand, so you must be aware of levels above your supply/demand levels So you see, there are many, many variables we must be aware of. In my opinion it is not only waiting for a break of the last low/high. We must learn to read the market: where the price is (HTF!!! LTF), where the price is probably going to, what is below/above a given level, is the level strong or it is tested? Maybe price just CP from your level not shot down with big momentum (very often hidden in the LTFs), we must take into account approach (demands/supplies tested on price's way? are there still fresh DPs?). Hopefully we can all learn something from me getting it wrong a little more Hey By, Make a note of why certain FTRs are marked and others are not in KKB's chart. Then ask yourself why is that? I did a study regarding the engulf of previous H and L to the left to get the new more difficult to fake edges of the new PAZ zones. This is not a magic bullet but it gives you a heads up to which FTRS are better as not all are created the same. For instance if we have engulfed a previous PAZ edge as in the chart above, made a new FTR and are heading up we should expect most of the FTRs to break on the way up until we get to our target origin i.e. the next PAZ edge not the next 4H FTR. It has to be made after engulfing another zone to the left. Thats why IF speaks of engulfing the bottom of poles. finding compression etc on a chart. It makes us aware of the zones that price is coming from and going to. There are only extremes i.e. the edges of our PAZ all the rest HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT is chop and prone to FOs. I would stick with HTF zones as if you try this on the Hourly or lower its a bit tricker to sort out and the big moves are coming off the HTFs anyways. Now you bring up a good point that what is midrange on the weekly can provide whopper scalps or daily swing trades. We just have to be aware that if price is bouncing from 1H demand to 1H supply while its on its way to 4H demand we can only expect definite pips to the next flag in the 1H TF. it could fake us and then go the other way or continue on past the 1H target but we have to keep an eye on PA: compression into demand, where could comp LQ go to, is this demand truly fresh, is there an untested flag directly below our entry, are there any patterns etc. There is a quote that I remember and recite daily from IF: Something to the effect that we need PA to prove that there is still supply or demand at any given level. Now we see wild 100:1 R:R trades taken from perfect 5 pip 1M levels but either PA was given on a larger TF or we are watching the approach like a Hawk and manage the trade as it progresses. The seniors I'm sure have a better grasp of PAZs than we do so they can pinpoint untested S/D at the edge of zones with great precision. We will be able to do this one day too. Sorry for my rambling hopefully this is helping. If you want to TT levels you have to weave all the TFs together to see if they are in sync. Then I have to have proof in the form of an engulf of a pole base with room to the next flag before I'll take a trade in a HTF zone. Yes I don't get 50 trades a week but I'm finding the ones I do get are much better quality. Cheers. I was trying to figure out the same initially and had written a small post of my understandings here (might help you a little ) www.readthemarket.com/index.php/forum/jo...ve-bulbuli-s-journal (Post # 13) Offline To put it simply 'Engulf' signifies consumption of orders and thus to have important/significant engulf, we must have a zone (significant) marked Trader where lot of pending orders are expected. But when this level is engulfed it shows that the orders have been consumed and thus price is free to If Life got a little bit go in the direction of engulf (After Retrace Of course !) The best ones we'll cover involve an Engulf Offline Simply put, an engulf involves price making a lower low on reaction to Supply, or higher high from Demand Chief Cook and Bottle Washer This is a very powerful sign that price is on the turn. They say that a trend is a succession of LL's and HH's, or vice versa, so if you get a Lower Low from Supply, it's a great first sign that the uptrend may be over, and of course flip the scenario for an HH from Demand. If you see a piece of truth glistening in the dirt, get down on your knees and gently dig it out, for it's the greatest treasure you will ever find Posts: 3576 Thank you received: 14517 If you didn't have the patience to wait for that engulf, see the Lower timeframe for another lovely engulf showing the way much earlier! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Can you trade an engulf as a breakout trade? Well, yes, but price very often retraces after the engulf, to confuse the breakout traders and hit their stop losses, creating more orders to take the other side of the big money's new direction trades. This means that the stop loss would, in most cases, still need to be beyond the original zone. Here's a video about the engulf https://mega.nz/#!xE4y1RDZ!7nKqcpGga4DyrfoqtiR70IFBuRYean8bS2fPCTeaTEA (passwo rd: RTM2018) So, in essence, it's as simple as this Two of our favourite Price Action structures based on the engulf are the Quasimodo and the Diamond The following user(s) said Thank You: aus1way, valtrg, pedrosa, gjv, bulbuli, Experiorz, fourtiwinks, khanahesan1985, Satan, bestgmx, Jurij, Enlightenme, Al3cs, salivan, pmbGUNNER, alirezat, Shantala, Canadough, Nytewyre, amateur.trader With time, patience and experience, what once seemed very complex becomes very simple indeed Please join www.theblue.community THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Engulf 26 Apr 2013 01:49 #2 HotochHiOnME In tEhDe UupCpAeTr lIeOvNel, how wFoOuRldUyMou knowMthOatRpErice wasLgOoGinOg UtoTretrace to a QM level?
